`frontend/src/modules/devices/DevicesReview.test.tsx`:

```tsx
/**
 * Waiting for review (install-link spec 2026-09-28 §1.5).
 *
 * A machine that joined through the install link is visible and held back. The
 * list says so, says how and from where it joined, and -- for a holder of
 * `devices.review` -- offers Approve, and Reject behind a confirmation that
 * says what Reject does. A Reject the agent cannot carry out itself tells the
 * technician to remove the agent by hand.
 */

import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { render, screen, within } from "@testing-library/react";
import userEvent from "@testing-library/user-event";
import { MemoryRouter, Route, Routes } from "react-router-dom";
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";

import type { ApiClient, Device, DeviceListParams, DevicePage, Me } from "../../lib/api";
import { DevicesPage } from "./DevicesPage";
import { joinedThroughLink, recordedByLabel } from "./reviewLabels";

const mockUseApi = vi.fn();
vi.mock("../../lib/ApiContext", () => ({ useApi: () => mockUseApi() }));
vi.mock("../../lib/ws", () => ({ useLiveEvents: () => undefined }));

function device(overrides: Partial<Device>): Device {
  return {
    id: "n1", hostname: "lis-ws-041", fqdn: null, os_family: "windows", os_version: "11 24H2",
    architecture: "amd64", device_group_id: null, status: "active", agent_status: "online",
    last_seen_at: "2026-09-28T09:00:00Z", cert_not_after: null, cert_state: "ok", cert_revoked: false,
    lifecycle: "in_service", asset_type: "computer", source: "agent", provenance: "agent",
    description: null, primary_ip: null, owner: null, location: null,
    review_state: "approved", link_joined_from: null, link_joined_at: null,
    ...overrides,
  };
}

const PENDING = device({
  id: "p1", hostname: "DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD", review_state: "pending",
  link_joined_from: "10.50.0.9", link_joined_at: new Date(Date.now() - 35 * 60_000).toISOString(),
});
const APPROVED = device({ id: "n1" });

function listFrom(estate: Device[]) {
  return vi.fn(async (params: DeviceListParams = {}): Promise<DevicePage> => {
    const pending = estate.filter((d) => d.review_state === "pending");
    const items = params.state === "review" ? pending : estate;
    return {
      items, total: items.length,
      state_counts: { all: estate.length, online: estate.length, offline: 0, assets: 0, certs: 0, review: pending.length },
      fleet: { total: estate.length, managed: estate.length, online: estate.length, cert_attention: 0 },
    };
  });
}

function renderDevices(api: Partial<ApiClient>, permissions: string[], path = "/devices?state=review") {
  mockUseApi.mockReturnValue({
    me: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ permissions } as Me),
    ...api,
  } as unknown as ApiClient);
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } });
  return render(
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <MemoryRouter initialEntries={[path]}>
        <Routes>
          <Route path="/devices" element={<DevicesPage />} />
        </Routes>
      </MemoryRouter>
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
}

beforeEach(() => mockUseApi.mockReset());

describe("Waiting for review", () => {
  it("has its own tab, and a row says how, from where and that it waits", async () => {
    renderDevices({ listDevices: listFrom([PENDING, APPROVED]) }, ["devices.read", "devices.review"]);
    const tab = await screen.findByRole("tab", { name: /Waiting for review/ });
    expect(tab).toHaveAttribute("aria-selected", "true");
    expect(tab).toHaveTextContent("1");
    const row = (await screen.findByText("DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD")).closest("tr")!;
    expect(row).toHaveTextContent("Joined through the install link from 10.50.0.9");
    expect(row).toHaveTextContent("Install link");
    expect(within(row).getByText("Waiting for review")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(within(row).queryByRole("button", { name: /Run job/ })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("approve asks the server and refreshes the list", async () => {
    const listDevices = listFrom([PENDING]);
    const approveDevice = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ ...PENDING, review_state: "approved" });
    renderDevices({ listDevices, approveDevice }, ["devices.read", "devices.review"]);
    await userEvent.click(await screen.findByRole("button", { name: "Approve DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD" }));
    expect(approveDevice).toHaveBeenCalledWith("p1");
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(listDevices.mock.calls.length).toBeGreaterThan(1));
  });

  it("reject asks first, says what it does, and passes on a manual-removal notice", async () => {
    const rejectDevice = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({
      device_id: "p1", uninstall: "manual", job_id: null,
      message: "This computer's agent is too old to remove itself. Its certificate has been revoked, so it can no longer connect; remove the agent from the computer by hand.",
    });
    renderDevices({ listDevices: listFrom([PENDING]), rejectDevice }, ["devices.read", "devices.review"]);
    await userEvent.click(await screen.findByRole("button", { name: "Reject DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD" }));
    let dialog = screen.getByRole("dialog", { name: "Reject this computer" });
    expect(dialog).toHaveTextContent("DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD");
    expect(dialog).toHaveTextContent("uninstall itself");
    await userEvent.click(within(dialog).getByRole("button", { name: "Cancel" }));
    expect(rejectDevice).not.toHaveBeenCalled();

    await userEvent.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Reject DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD" }));
    dialog = screen.getByRole("dialog", { name: "Reject this computer" });
    await userEvent.click(within(dialog).getByRole("button", { name: "Reject and uninstall" }));
    expect(rejectDevice).toHaveBeenCalledWith("p1");
    expect(await screen.findByRole("status")).toHaveTextContent("remove the agent from the computer by hand");
  });

  it("without the review permission the row waits and offers nothing", async () => {
    renderDevices({ listDevices: listFrom([PENDING]) }, ["devices.read"]);
    const row = (await screen.findByText("DESKTOP-7Q2M4KD")).closest("tr")!;
    expect(within(row).queryByRole("button", { name: /Approve|Reject/ })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(row).toHaveTextContent("Waiting for someone who can approve or reject new computers");
  });
});

describe("reviewLabels", () => {
  it("names how a row joined in the operator's words", () => {
    expect(joinedThroughLink(PENDING)).toBe("Joined through the install link from 10.50.0.9");
    expect(joinedThroughLink({ ...PENDING, link_joined_from: null })).toBe("Joined through the install link");
    expect(joinedThroughLink(APPROVED)).toBeNull();
    expect(recordedByLabel(PENDING, "Agent enrolment")).toBe("Install link");
    expect(recordedByLabel(APPROVED, "Agent enrolment")).toBe("Agent enrolment");
  });
});
```

```tsx
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { renderHook, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
import type { ReactNode } from "react";
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";

import type { ApiClient } from "../lib/api";
import { useReviewCount } from "./useReviewCount";

const mockUseApi = vi.fn();
vi.mock("../lib/ApiContext", () => ({ useApi: () => mockUseApi() }));

function wrapper({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const client = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } });
  return <QueryClientProvider client={client}>{children}</QueryClientProvider>;
}

beforeEach(() => mockUseApi.mockReset());

describe("useReviewCount", () => {
  it("is the server's review count for someone who can act on it", async () => {
    const listDevices = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({
      items: [], total: 3,
      state_counts: { all: 40, online: 30, offline: 10, assets: 0, certs: 0, review: 3 },
      fleet: { total: 40, managed: 40, online: 30, cert_attention: 0 },
    });
    mockUseApi.mockReturnValue({ listDevices } as unknown as ApiClient);
    const { result } = renderHook(() => useReviewCount(["devices.read", "devices.review"]), { wrapper });
    await waitFor(() => expect(result.current).toBe(3));
    expect(listDevices).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ state: "review", limit: 1 });
  });

  it("asks nothing and shows nothing for anyone else", () => {
    const listDevices = vi.fn();
    mockUseApi.mockReturnValue({ listDevices } as unknown as ApiClient);
    const { result } = renderHook(() => useReviewCount(["devices.read"]), { wrapper });
    expect(result.current).toBeNull();
    expect(listDevices).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });
});
```

```ts
/** What a Reject did (install-link spec §1.5, §4). */
export interface DeviceRejected {
  device_id: string;
  /** `manual`: the agent is too old to remove itself; a technician must. */
  uninstall: "dispatched" | "manual";
  job_id: string | null;
  /** One sentence for the technician. Rendered as given. */
  message: string;
}
```

```ts
/**
 * Waiting for review, in the operator's words (install-link spec §1.5).
 * `review_state` is a wire value and never reaches the screen as itself.
 */

import type { Device } from "../../lib/api";

export const REVIEW_STATE_LABELS: Record<NonNullable<Device["review_state"]>, string> = {
  approved: "Approved",
  pending: "Waiting for review",
  rejected: "Rejected",
};

export function isWaitingForReview(device: Device): boolean {
  return device.review_state === "pending";
}

/** "Joined through the install link from 10.50.0.9", or null for any other row. */
export function joinedThroughLink(device: Device): string | null {
  if (!device.link_joined_at) return null;
  return device.link_joined_from
    ? `Joined through the install link from ${device.link_joined_from}`
    : "Joined through the install link";
}

/** The "Recorded by" cell: the install link names itself; everything else keeps its label. */
export function recordedByLabel(device: Device, fallback: string): string {
  return device.link_joined_at ? "Install link" : fallback;
}
```

`frontend/src/modules/devices/ReviewActions.tsx`:

```tsx
/**
 * Approve and Reject for a computer waiting for review (install-link spec §1.5).
 *
 * REJECT IS CONFIRMED, AND THE CONFIRMATION SAYS WHAT IT DOES: the agent is
 * told to uninstall itself, the certificate is revoked, the row leaves the
 * list, and nothing brings it back but installing the agent again. The notice
 * a Reject returns is handed UP (`onRejected`) because the row -- and this
 * component -- disappear the moment the list refreshes.
 *
 * INVARIANT 6: the page renders this only for a holder of `devices.review`;
 * both routes check it themselves.
 */

import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useState } from "react";

import { ConfirmDialog } from "../../components/confirm";
import { describeSubmitError } from "../../components/form";
import type { Device } from "../../lib/api";
import { useApi } from "../../lib/ApiContext";
import { Btn } from "../../shell/components";

export const REVIEW_PERMISSION = "devices.review";

export function ReviewActions({
  device,
  label,
  onRejected,
}: {
  device: Device;
  /** The name the operator recognises the row by. */
  label: string;
  onRejected: (message: string) => void;
}) {
  const api = useApi();
  const client = useQueryClient();
  const [confirming, setConfirming] = useState(false);
  // `["devices"]` is a prefix of both the list and the nav count's key.
  const refresh = () => void client.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["devices"] });
  const approve = useMutation({ mutationFn: () => api.approveDevice(device.id), onSuccess: refresh });
  const reject = useMutation({
    mutationFn: () => api.rejectDevice(device.id),
    onSuccess: (result) => {
      onRejected(result.message);
      refresh();
    },
  });
  const busy = approve.isPending || reject.isPending;
  const failure = approve.error ?? reject.error;

  return (
    <div className="row-actions">
      <Btn small variant="primary" label="Approve" ariaLabel={`Approve ${label}`} disabled={busy} onClick={() => approve.mutate()} />
      <Btn small variant="danger" label="Reject" ariaLabel={`Reject ${label}`} disabled={busy} onClick={() => setConfirming(true)} />
      {failure ? (
        <p role="alert" className="form-error">
          {describeSubmitError(failure)}
        </p>
      ) : null}
      <ConfirmDialog
        open={confirming}
        title="Reject this computer"
        subject={label}
        body="Its agent is told to uninstall itself and its certificate is revoked, so it can no longer connect. It leaves Devices now. This cannot be undone: to bring it back, install the agent again."
        confirmLabel="Reject and uninstall"
        destructive
        onConfirm={() => {
          setConfirming(false);
          reject.mutate();
        }}
        onCancel={() => setConfirming(false)}
      />
    </div>
  );
}
```

`frontend/src/shell/useReviewCount.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * THE NAV COUNT FOR DEVICES: computers waiting for review (install-link spec
 * §1.5). The server's own `state_counts.review`, asked for with a one-row page
 * so the count costs one aggregate, not a list. Only for someone who can act
 * on it (`devices.review`) and read the list (`devices.read`); for anyone else
 * nothing is asked and nothing is shown -- `null`, which the rail renders as
 * no badge, and which is not the same claim as zero.
 *
 * The key starts with `"devices"`, so every Approve and Reject (which
 * invalidate that prefix) refreshes it.
 */

import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query";

import { useApi } from "../lib/ApiContext";

export const REVIEW_COUNT_KEY = ["devices", "review-count"] as const;

export function useReviewCount(permissions: readonly string[]): number | null {
  const api = useApi();
  const enabled = permissions.includes("devices.read") && permissions.includes("devices.review");
  const count = useQuery({
    queryKey: REVIEW_COUNT_KEY,
    queryFn: () => api.listDevices({ state: "review", limit: 1 }),
    enabled,
    staleTime: 30_000,
    refetchInterval: 60_000,
  });
  if (!enabled || count.data == null) return null;
  return count.data.state_counts.review ?? null;
}
```

### Task 16: The two beats — the link's automatic switch-off, and completing rejections

**Files:**
- Modify: `backend/app/services/install_link.py` (one constant, `AUTO_OFF_INTERVAL_S`)
- Modify: `backend/app/worker.py` (imports; `install_link_auto_off_all_orgs`, `review_rejections_all_orgs`; two Celery tasks; two beat entries)
- Test: `backend/tests/test_review_beats.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `install_link.switch_off_due(session, org_id, now) -> bool` (Task 3); `device_review.complete_due_rejections(session, org_id, *, now) -> list[CompletedRejection]`, `device_review.COMPLETION_INTERVAL_S` (Task 9); `app.gateway.dispatch.publish_revoke`; `app.core.ca.get_ca`.
- Produces: `install_link.AUTO_OFF_INTERVAL_S = 60.0`; `worker.install_link_auto_off_all_orgs(now: datetime) -> int`; `worker.review_rejections_all_orgs(now: datetime) -> int`; Celery tasks `app.worker.install_link_auto_off`, `app.worker.device_review_completion`; beat entries `"install-link-auto-off"` and `"device-review-completion"`, both every 60 s.

The link already stops answering at `disable_at` on every request (`install_link.is_live`); the auto-off beat is what RECORDS it — the switch's visible state and the `enrollment.link_disabled` audit row with `reason: scheduled` (spec §1.1). The completion beat is spec §1.5's "once the agent confirms or after 24 hours, whichever is first" (plan D2).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`backend/tests/test_review_beats.py`:

```python
"""The install link's two beats (spec 2026-09-28 §1.1 automatic switch-off,
§1.5 rejection completion; plan D1, D2)."""

from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any
from uuid import UUID, uuid4

import pytest
from sqlalchemy import func, select
from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncEngine, AsyncSession

from app.core.ca import LocalCA
from app.core.config import Settings, override_settings
from app.models import Agent, AuditEvent, Device, InstallLink, Job, JobStatus
from app.services import device_review, install_link
from tests.ipam_fixtures import seed_agent, seed_device, seed_org

NOW = datetime(2026, 9, 28, 12, 0, tzinfo=UTC)


def _settings(pg_cluster: Any, tmp_path: Path) -> Settings:
    return Settings(
        database_url=pg_cluster.app_url,
        database_admin_url=pg_cluster.admin_url,
        oidc_issuer="https://kc.example/realms/it-suite",
        oidc_jwks_url="https://kc.example/realms/it-suite/certs",
        ca_dir=tmp_path / "ca",
        signing_key_path=tmp_path / "job-signing.key",
    )


async def _count(admin_engine: AsyncEngine, org_id: UUID, action: str) -> int:
    async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
        return int(
            (
                await session.execute(
                    select(func.count()).select_from(AuditEvent).where(
                        AuditEvent.org_id == org_id, AuditEvent.action == action
                    )
                )
            ).scalar_one()
        )


async def test_the_auto_off_beat_records_a_passed_switch_off_once(
    admin_engine: AsyncEngine, pg_cluster: Any, tmp_path: Path
) -> None:
    from app import worker

    due = (await seed_org(admin_engine, "ao")).org_id
    later = (await seed_org(admin_engine, "ao2")).org_id
    async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
        session.add_all(
            [
                InstallLink(org_id=due, enabled=True, code_hash=uuid4().hex * 2,
                            networks=["10.0.0.0/8"], disable_at=NOW - timedelta(minutes=1),
                            enabled_at=NOW - timedelta(hours=3)),
                InstallLink(org_id=later, enabled=True, code_hash=uuid4().hex * 2,
                            networks=["10.0.0.0/8"], disable_at=NOW + timedelta(days=1),
                            enabled_at=NOW - timedelta(hours=3)),
            ]
        )
        await session.commit()

    with override_settings(_settings(pg_cluster, tmp_path)):
        await worker.install_link_auto_off_all_orgs(NOW)
        await worker.install_link_auto_off_all_orgs(NOW + timedelta(minutes=1))

    async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
        rows = {
            r.org_id: r
            for r in (await session.execute(select(InstallLink).where(InstallLink.org_id.in_([due, later])))).scalars()
        }
        audit = (
            await session.execute(
                select(AuditEvent).where(
                    AuditEvent.org_id == due, AuditEvent.action == "enrollment.link_disabled"
                )
            )
        ).scalars().all()
    assert rows[due].enabled is False and rows[due].enabled_at is None
    assert rows[later].enabled is True
    assert len(audit) == 1 and audit[0].meta["reason"] == "scheduled"
    assert await _count(admin_engine, later, "enrollment.link_disabled") == 0


async def _rejected(admin_engine: AsyncEngine, *, hours_ago: float, confirmed: bool) -> tuple[UUID, UUID, str]:
    org = await seed_org(admin_engine, "rj")
    device_id = await seed_device(
        admin_engine, org.org_id, review_state="rejected",
        rejected_at=NOW - timedelta(hours=hours_ago),
    )
    await seed_agent(admin_engine, org.org_id, device_id, capabilities={"agent.uninstall": True})
    async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
        serial = (await session.execute(select(Agent.cert_serial).where(Agent.device_id == device_id))).scalar_one()
        if confirmed:
            session.add(
                Job(org_id=org.org_id, type="agent.uninstall", target_type="device",
                    target_id=device_id, params={}, nonce=uuid4().hex,
                    issued_at=NOW - timedelta(hours=1), expires_at=NOW + timedelta(hours=23),
                    status=JobStatus.succeeded, finished_at=NOW - timedelta(minutes=30),
                    result={"scheduled": True})
            )
        await session.commit()
    return org.org_id, device_id, serial


async def test_a_rejection_completes_when_the_agent_confirms_or_after_24_hours(
    admin_engine: AsyncEngine, pg_cluster: Any, tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
) -> None:
    from app import worker

    confirmed = await _rejected(admin_engine, hours_ago=1, confirmed=True)
    timed_out = await _rejected(admin_engine, hours_ago=25, confirmed=False)
    waiting = await _rejected(admin_engine, hours_ago=1, confirmed=False)

    revoked_connections: list[UUID] = []

    async def fake_publish_revoke(_redis: Any, device_id: UUID) -> None:
        revoked_connections.append(device_id)

    monkeypatch.setattr(worker, "publish_revoke", fake_publish_revoke)
    with override_settings(_settings(pg_cluster, tmp_path)):
        await worker.review_rejections_all_orgs(NOW)
        await worker.review_rejections_all_orgs(NOW + timedelta(minutes=1))  # idempotent

    ca = LocalCA(tmp_path / "ca")
    async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
        for org_id, device_id, serial in (confirmed, timed_out):
            device = await session.get(Device, device_id)
            agent = (await session.execute(select(Agent).where(Agent.device_id == device_id))).scalar_one()
            assert device is not None and device.status == "decommissioned"
            assert agent.cert_revoked is True
            assert ca.is_revoked(serial)
            assert device_id in revoked_connections
            assert await _count(admin_engine, org_id, "device.rejection_completed") == 1
        still = await session.get(Device, waiting[1])
        still_agent = (await session.execute(select(Agent).where(Agent.device_id == waiting[1]))).scalar_one()
    assert still is not None and still.status != "decommissioned" and still_agent.cert_revoked is False
    assert waiting[1] not in revoked_connections

    async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
        reasons = {
            e.target_id: e.meta["reason"]
            for e in (
                await session.execute(
                    select(AuditEvent).where(AuditEvent.action == "device.rejection_completed",
                                             AuditEvent.org_id.in_([confirmed[0], timed_out[0]]))
                )
            ).scalars()
        }
    assert reasons == {str(confirmed[1]): "agent_confirmed", str(timed_out[1]): "timeout"}


def test_both_beats_are_scheduled_every_minute() -> None:
    from app import worker

    schedule = worker.create_celery().conf.beat_schedule
    assert schedule["install-link-auto-off"]["task"] == "app.worker.install_link_auto_off"
    assert schedule["install-link-auto-off"]["schedule"] == install_link.AUTO_OFF_INTERVAL_S == 60.0
    assert schedule["device-review-completion"]["task"] == "app.worker.device_review_completion"
    assert schedule["device-review-completion"]["schedule"] == device_review.COMPLETION_INTERVAL_S == 60.0
```

```python
from app.services import device_review as device_review_module
```

After `subscription_alerts_all_orgs`:

```python
async def install_link_auto_off_all_orgs(now: datetime) -> int:
    """Record every install link whose automatic switch-off has passed
    (install-link spec §1.1): `enabled` false and one `enrollment.link_disabled`
    audit row with `reason: scheduled`. The link stopped ANSWERING at
    `disable_at` already -- `install_link.is_live` checks it on every request --
    so a late beat delays the record, never the switch-off. One org's failure is
    contained; see `_OrgCycleFailures`."""
    settings = get_settings()
    app_engine = create_async_engine(settings.database_url)
    admin_engine = create_async_engine(settings.database_admin_url)
    failures = _OrgCycleFailures("install_link.auto_off")
    total = 0
    try:
        async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
            org_ids = list((await session.execute(select(Organization.id))).scalars())
        for org_id in org_ids:
            try:
                async with org_session(org_id, engine=app_engine) as session:
                    if await install_link_module.switch_off_due(session, org_id, now):
                        total += 1
            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - contained, see `_OrgCycleFailures`
                failures.contain(org_id, exc)
        failures.raise_if_total(len(org_ids))
    finally:
        await app_engine.dispose()
        await admin_engine.dispose()
    return total


async def review_rejections_all_orgs(now: datetime) -> int:
    """Complete every rejection whose agent confirmed its uninstall, or whose 24
    hours are up (install-link spec §1.5, plan D2): revoke the certificate, make
    the row a `decommissioned` tombstone, audit `device.rejection_completed`.

    THE ORDER IS `POST /agents/{id}/revoke`'s: the DB flag (the gateway's
    admission gate) commits inside the tenant transaction; only then the CRL
    and the live-connection drop. A CRL write that fails is retried by nothing
    automatically -- but the device is already refused at the gateway, which
    is the fail-safe direction."""
    from app.core.ca import get_ca

    settings = get_settings()
    app_engine = create_async_engine(settings.database_url)
    admin_engine = create_async_engine(settings.database_admin_url)
    redis = aioredis.from_url(settings.redis_url, decode_responses=True)
    ca = get_ca(settings)
    failures = _OrgCycleFailures("device_review.completion")
    total = 0
    try:
        async with AsyncSession(admin_engine) as session:
            org_ids = list((await session.execute(select(Organization.id))).scalars())
        for org_id in org_ids:
            try:
                async with org_session(org_id, engine=app_engine) as session:
                    done = await device_review_module.complete_due_rejections(
                        session, org_id, now=now
                    )
                # COMMITTED. Now the CRL, then the connection.
                for item in done:
                    if item.revoke_serial is not None:
                        await ca.revoke(item.revoke_serial)
                    await publish_revoke(redis, item.device_id)
                total += len(done)
            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - contained, see `_OrgCycleFailures`
                failures.contain(org_id, exc)
        failures.raise_if_total(len(org_ids))
    finally:
        await redis.aclose()
        await app_engine.dispose()
        await admin_engine.dispose()
    return total
```

In the beat schedule dict passed to `_with_expiry`, after `"subscription-alerts"`:

```python
        # The install link (spec 2026-09-28 §1.1, §1.5). Both every minute:
        # the switch-off is already enforced per request, and a rejection's
        # certificate should not outlive the agent's confirmation by long.
        "install-link-auto-off": {
            "task": "app.worker.install_link_auto_off",
            "schedule": install_link_module.AUTO_OFF_INTERVAL_S,
        },
        "device-review-completion": {
            "task": "app.worker.device_review_completion",
            "schedule": device_review_module.COMPLETION_INTERVAL_S,
        },
```

and beside the `subscription_alerts` task:

- [ ] **Step 3: Amend the packaging README**

`agent/packaging/README.md`: in the table under "The one thing to get right", the **A distributable artifact** row's second cell gains a final sentence: `The install link is the one recorded exception, and it puts a code in a file's NAME, never in its bytes — see the next section.` Then add, directly before `## Build`:

```markdown
## The install link: a code in a file name (operator decision, 2026-09-28)

`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-install-link-and-windows-push-design.md`
§0 item 1 records the operator overturning the third row above for one
purpose: a technician opens the organisation's **install link** on the
machine, and the agent installs itself with no token to paste.

**What changed, exactly.** The bytes did not: the Windows download is the
published artefact, byte-identical for every tenant, and its release signature
(and, later, Authenticode) stays valid. The **link code** rides in the
downloaded file's **name**, `ITMS-Agent-Setup_<code>.exe`, and on macOS and
Linux in the one-line `install.sh` the page shows. A link code is not an
enrollment token: it enrols a machine only while the link is on, only from a
network the operator listed, only under the hourly limit — and what it enrols
is a machine **Waiting for review**, which receives inventory, health
monitoring and certificate renewal and nothing else until a technician
approves it.

**What did not change.**

- **argv (row 1).** The code is never an argument. The Windows self-installer
  reads it from its own file name; its UAC re-launch carries only
  `--elevated-install`; the service is registered with no arguments.
  `install.sh` hands the code to `install.sh --token-stdin` through the shell's
  own `printf`.
- **A service definition (row 2).** The service's environment holds the data
  directory and the console URL, never the code. The code goes to the ordinary
  token file, SYSTEM and Administrators only (Windows) or `0600` (unix), and
  the agent deletes it the moment it has a certificate.

**What remains, stated plainly.**

1. The file stays in the **Downloads folder** of every machine installed this
   way, name and all, until someone deletes it.
2. The file name is signed into the object store's presigned URL
   (`response-content-disposition`), so it appears in the **object-store access
   log** if that log is enabled.
3. While the installer runs, the code is in its own image path — `argv[0]` —
   because that is the file's name. No long-lived process ever carries it.

All three are bounded by the same controls (spec §1.6): the network list, the
hourly limit, the on/off switch, the optional automatic switch-off, and
**regeneration**, which makes every copy of the old code worthless at once.

**How the Windows installer knows which console to join.** Not from the code
and not from the bytes: from the file's **Mark of the Web**. Edge and Chrome
record the page a download came from as `ReferrerUrl` in
`<file>:Zone.Identifier`, and the link page sends `Referrer-Policy: origin`,
so that value is the console's origin. A file with no Mark of the Web (copied
from a share, downloaded with a browser that does not write one, or
"unblocked") refuses to install and says to download it again with Edge or
Chrome.
```

```markdown
**Amended 2026-09-28 (operator's decision, install-link spec §0 item 1).**
"Artefacts are token-free and byte-identical for every tenant" still holds for
the BYTES. The install link puts a link code in the downloaded file's NAME
(`ITMS-Agent-Setup_<code>.exe`) and in the page's one-line `install.sh`, where
it stays in the Downloads folder of every machine installed that way. A link
code is not an enrollment token — it enrols only while the link is on, only
from a listed network, under an hourly limit, and only into *Waiting for
review* — and the exposure is bounded by spec §1.6 and recorded in full in
`agent/packaging/README.md`, "The install link: a code in a file name".
```

and §7's last bullet becomes:

```markdown
  Every artefact read is a presigned, time-bounded grant minted for an

  download and its `install.sh` (`/i/<code>`), which are public by the

  only while the link is on, only a request from a listed network, and
  otherwise exactly the 404 of a path that does not exist; the download is
  still a 15-minute presigned grant, minted by the server for that request.
```

**There is no in-tree agent version constant to bump.** The agent's version is stamped at release time (`-X main.version`, from `release-agent.yml`'s `version` input); the only version floor in this PR is Task 7's `LINK_MIN_AGENT_VERSION = 1.6.0`, and the `agent.uninstall` gate is a capability check, not a version check (Task 9). "Agent 1.6.0" therefore means: cut release **1.6.0** from the merged `main` and mark it stable (Appendix B). ## Appendix A — the deploy sequence for PR 1 (install link)

**This starts after the PR is merged to `main`.** Every step is listed, including the ones that are no-ops for this change.

What this release contains, for the operator: **one new migration (`0170`)**; **one new `.env` key, `ITS_TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS`**, whose default is correct for the shipped compose file (leave it unset); a Traefik route for `/i/` on the `api` service; **two new beat entries** (`install-link-auto-off`, `device-review-completion`). The install link is **off** after the deploy and stays off until someone switches it on.

```sh
# 0. On the host, in the checkout that runs the stack.
cd /opt/it-suite/deploy

# 1. Is OpenBao sealed? It is sealed after EVERY restart and EVERY host reboot,
#    and a sealed OpenBao makes `up -d` BLOCK rather than fail. Whether it is
#    sealed now depends on this host's history, which nobody here can see.
docker compose exec openbao bao status
#    If it reports "Sealed  true", unseal with three shares, one per command:
docker compose exec openbao bao operator unseal
docker compose exec openbao bao operator unseal
docker compose exec openbao bao operator unseal

# 2. Record what is running, and back up. THERE IS A NEW MIGRATION (0170), and
#    migrations are forward-only: going back past it is restore-from-backup.
grep IT_SUITE_VERSION .env
docker image ls itsuite/backend --format '{{.Tag}}'
./scripts/backup.sh ~/backups/pre-install-link-$(date -u +%FT%H%MZ)
#    Copy the directory it printed OFF this host (it contains role password hashes).

# 3. Pull main.
git checkout main
git pull

# 4. The new .env key. NO ACTION FOR THE SHIPPED COMPOSE FILE: unset, the api
#    trusts X-Forwarded-For only from ITMS_NETWORK_SUBNET, the network Traefik
#    sits on. This prints the line if you ever set it, or says it is unset.
grep -n '^ITS_TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS=' .env || echo "ITS_TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS unset: default applies (correct for the shipped compose file)"
#    Recommended, not required: the install link is built from
#    ITS_PUBLIC_CONSOLE_URL when it is set. This shows its current value.
grep -n '^ITS_PUBLIC_CONSOLE_URL=' .env

# 5. Build the images from main, tagged with the commit, and select that tag.
./scripts/build-images.sh
NEW_VERSION=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)
grep -q '^IT_SUITE_VERSION=' .env \
  && sed -i "s/^IT_SUITE_VERSION=.*/IT_SUITE_VERSION=$NEW_VERSION/" .env \
  || echo "IT_SUITE_VERSION=$NEW_VERSION" >> .env
grep IT_SUITE_VERSION .env      # exactly one line, with the new SHA

# 6. Migrate (0170: install_links, devices.review_state and two permission
#    codes), then the idempotent one-shots.
docker compose up migrate
docker compose up postgres-grants     # idempotent; install_links is covered by default privileges
docker compose up openbao-init        # idempotent; nothing new in this release
docker compose up keycloak-init       # idempotent; nothing new in this release

# 7. Restart. This recreates `api` (new image, new Traefik rule for /i/) and
#    `worker` (the two new beat entries are read at worker start).
docker compose up -d

# 8. Verify.
docker compose ps -a --format 'table {{.Service}}\t{{.Status}}'
docker compose exec postgres sh -c 'PGPASSWORD="$POSTGRES_PASSWORD" psql -h localhost -U its_admin -d itsuite -tAc "select version_num from alembic_version;"'
#    -> 0170
docker compose exec api python -c "from app.core.config import get_settings; print(get_settings().trusted_proxy_cidrs)"
#    -> 172.19.240.0/24 (or your ITMS_NETWORK_SUBNET)
docker compose exec worker python -c \
  "from app.worker import create_celery; s = create_celery().conf.beat_schedule; print('install-link-auto-off' in s, 'device-review-completion' in s)"
#    -> True True
docker compose exec api python -c \
  "import httpx; print(httpx.get('http://localhost:8000/i/AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA').status_code)"
#    -> 404: the link is off, and an off link is indistinguishable from no link.
```

**Rolling back.** Migration 0170 only adds (a table, four columns with defaults, two permission rows), so the previous application image runs against it unchanged. If step 2's `docker image ls` printed an earlier tag, rolling back is retagging to it:

```sh
cd /opt/it-suite/deploy
PREVIOUS=$(docker image ls itsuite/backend --format '{{.Tag}}' | grep -v "^$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)$" | grep -v '^dev$' | head -n 1)
echo "rolling back to: $PREVIOUS"      # read it; if it is empty, stop here -- see below
sed -i "s/^IT_SUITE_VERSION=.*/IT_SUITE_VERSION=$PREVIOUS/" .env
docker compose up -d
```

If that list held no earlier tag (`(none yet)` from `build-images.sh`, or only `dev`), **there is no image to roll back to**, and the only way back is restoring step 2's backup (`./scripts/restore.sh` with the directory it printed, per `docs/07-backup-restore.md`).

**Then, in the console**, when the operator wants the link: *Agent packages → Install link* → list the networks technicians install from → *Accept new computers through the link* → *Save install link* → copy the link shown once. It offers nothing until Appendix B's release is stable.

## Appendix B — cutting agent 1.6.0 (after Appendix A)

The link page offers only a **stable** release **1.6.0 or later**, and `agent.uninstall` exists only in agents built from this PR. From a clone with `gh` signed in:

```sh
VERSION=1.6.0
gh workflow run release-agent.yml --ref main -f version="$VERSION"
gh run watch
gh release download "agent-v$VERSION" --dir "its-agent-$VERSION"
ls "its-agent-$VERSION"
```

Then in the console: *Agent packages → Publish a release…* with version `1.6.0` and the files just downloaded (`SIGNATURES` included), then *Mark as stable…* for 1.6.0. Verify: open the install link from an allowed network — the page offers *Download for Windows* and the macOS/Linux command. Existing agents move to 1.6.0 through the ordinary self-update rollout (*Agent packages → Agent updates*); until an agent is on 1.6.0, rejecting it revokes its certificate and asks for manual removal, as designed (spec §4).
